Set-valued tableaux for Macdonald polynomials
Zajj Daugherty, email: zdaugherty@gmail.com, email: aram@unimelb.edu.au

Abstract
Set-valued tableaux formulas play an important role in Schubert calculus. Using the box greedy reduced word for the construction of the Macdonald polynomials, we convert the alcove walk formula for Macdonald polynomials to a set-valued tableaux formula for Macdonald polynomials. Our hope is that providing set-valued tableaux formulas for Macdonald polynomials will help to strengthen the analogies and possible connections between the calculus of Macdonald polynomials and Schubert calculus.



In memory of Georgia Benkart

Key words—
Macdonald polynomials, tableaux formulas, Schubert calculus

1
1

1

AMS Subject Classifications: Primary 05E05; Secondary 33D52.

1
Introduction

The goal of this paper is to give set-valued tableaux formulas for Macdonald polynomials.
As evidenced by the work of
[
Bu02
]
,
[
KMY09
]
,
[
MS19
]
,
[
PS19
]
,
[
Wei20
]
,
[
Yu21
]
and others,
set-valued tableaux formulas play an important role in
Schubert calculus and our hope is that providing set-valued tableaux formulas for Macdonald polynomials will help
to strengthen the analogies and possible connections between the calculus
of Macdonald polynomials and Schubert calculus.

Our set-valued tableaux formulas for Macdonald polynomials
are derived by making a bijection between set-valued tableaux
with alcove walks. Using this bijection and converting the alcove walks formula from
[
RY08
, Theorem 2.2]

produces a set-valued tableaux formula for Macdonald polynomials.
We follow the framework of
[
GR21
, §1 and §2]
, which gives an exposition of the alcove walks formula
in the
GL_{n}
context and analyzes the favourite reduced for the
n
-periodic permutation
u_{\mu}

that is used to construct the relative Macdonald polynomial
E^{z}_{\mu}
.

Providing a set-valued tableaux formula for
E^{z}_{\mu}
also provides a set-valued tableau formula for
the symmetric Macdonald polynomials
P_{\lambda}
since if
\lambda
is a partition then
(see
[
GR21b
, (1.4),(1.5) and (1.10)]
)

P_{\lambda}=(const)\sum_{z\in S_{n}\lambda}E^{z}_{\lambda}.

The set-valued tableaux formulas given in Theorem
2.1
(respectively, Theorem
2.2
) are specializable
at
q=0
and
t=0
(respectively,
q=\infty
and
t=\infty
).
The specializations of
E^{z}_{\mu}
at
t=0
and
t=\infty
have interpretations in terms of crystals
for level 1 and level 0 affine Demazure characters (see, for example,
[
Kt16
, Theorem C and Corollary D]

or
[
MRY19
, (2.12), Theorem 1.1 and §2.4]
).
It would be interesting to write the root operators for these crystals explicitly on the set-valued tableaux
(it is likely that the crystals of
[
Yu21
, §4]
cover some cases of these crystals).

2
Set-valued tableaux for Macdonald polynomials

We begin with combinatorial definitions necessary for stating the set-valued tableaux formula
for Macdonald polynomials.
To aid in processing these definitions, we encourage the reader to follow
Example
2.1
in tandem.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and let
S_{n}
denote the symmetric group on
n
letters.
Identify
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}

with
the set of boxes in
\mu
,

\mu=\{(r,c)\ |\ \hbox{$r\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and $c\in\{1,\ldots,\mu_{r}\}$}\}.

2.1
Definition of the shift and height statistics

The minimal length permutation
v_{\mu}\in S_{n}
such that
v_{\mu}\mu
is weakly increasing
is given by

v_{\mu}(r)=1+\#\{r^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}\ |\ \mu_{r^{\prime}}\leq\mu_{r}\}+\#\{r^{\prime}\in\{r+1,\ldots,n\}\ |\ \mu_{r^{\prime}}<\mu_{r}\},

for
r\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
For a box
(r,c)\in\mu
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,u_{\mu}(r,c)\}
define

\mathrm{sh}(r,c)=\mu_{r}-c+1\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathrm{ht}(i,r,c)=v_{\mu}(r)-i.

(2.1)

2.2
Definition of set-valued tableaux

For
(r,c)\in\mu
define

u_{\mu}(r,c)=\#\{r^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,r-1\}\ |\ \mu_{r^{\prime}}<c\leq\mu_{r}\}+\#\{r^{\prime}\in\{r+1,\ldots,n\}\ |\ \mu_{r^{\prime}}<c-1<\mu_{r}\}.

(2.2)

The values
u_{\mu}(r,c)
play a similar role to the flagging in the use of set-valued tableaux to give
formulas for Grothendieck polynomials (see
[
Wei20
, §2.6 and Theorem 7.1]
).
A
set-valued tableau
T
of shape
\mu

is a choice of subset
T(r,c)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,u_{\mu}(r,c)\}

for each box
(r,c)\in\mu
. More formally, a set-valued tableau
T
of shape
\mu

is a function

T\colon\mu\to\{\hbox{subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$}\}\qquad\hbox{such that}\quad T(r,c)\subseteq\{1,\ldots,u_{\mu}(r,c)\}.

Let
|T|
denote the total number of entries in
T
.

2.3
Definition of
z^{T}_{(r,c)}
and
x^{T}

Using one-line notation, let
\gamma_{n}
be the
n
-cycle in the symmetric group
S_{n}
given by

\gamma_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}n&1&2&\cdots&n-1\end{pmatrix}.

For positive integers
k_{1},\ldots,k_{\ell}
such that
k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\ell}=n
, let

\gamma_{k_{1}}\times\cdots\times\gamma_{k_{\ell}}\qquad\hbox{be the disjoint
product of cycles in}\qquad S_{k_{1}}\times\cdots\times S_{k_{\ell}}\subseteq S_{n}.

Fix a permutation
z\in S_{n}
.
Order the boxes of
\mu
down columns and then left to right (i.e., in increasing order of the values
r+nc
).
Starting from the permutation
z
,
associate a permutation
z^{T}_{(r,c)}
to each box
(r,c)
of
\mu
as follows: If
(r,c)\in\mu

and
T(r,c)=\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\}
with
1\leq m_{1}<\cdots<m_{p}\leq u_{\mu}(r,c)

define

z_{(r,c)}^{T}=z_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}^{T}(\gamma_{m_{1}-0}\times\gamma_{m_{2}-m_{1}}\cdots\times\gamma_{m_{p}-m_{p-1}}\times\gamma_{u_{\mu}(r,c)+1-m_{p}}\times(\gamma_{1})^{\times(n-u_{\mu}(r,c)-1)})\gamma_{n}^{-1},

(2.3)

where
(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in\mu
denotes the box before
(r,c)
in
\mu
.
In the case that
(r,c)
is the first box in
\mu
we let
z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}=z
.
Then define

x^{T}=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}x_{z^{T}_{(r,c)}(n)},

(2.4)

where
z^{T}_{(r,c)}(n)
is the value of the permutation
z^{T}_{(r,c)}
at
n
.

The permutation
z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}=z
is the
left key
(or
initial direction
) and
the permutation
z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}}
corresponding to the last box in
\mu

is the
right key
(or
final direction
).
These left and right keys play important roles in Schubert calculus: for example in

[
Yu21
, §3]
and in the statement of Pieri-Chevalley formulas
(compare
[
FL94
, Theorem 1]
and
[
PR98
, Corollary]
).

2.4
Definition of the cov and maj statistics.

Keep the notation
T(r,c)=\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{p}\}
with
m_{1}<\ldots<m_{p}
.
Define

\displaystyle T^{z}_{<}(r,c)

\displaystyle=\{m_{k}\in T(r,c)\ |\ z^{T}_{(r,c)}(m_{k-1})<z^{T}_{(r,c)}(m_{k})\}\qquad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle T^{z}_{>}(r,c)

\displaystyle=\{m_{k}\in T(r,c)\ |\ z^{T}_{(r,c)}(m_{k-1})>z^{T}_{(r,c)}(m_{k})\},

(2.5)

where we make the convention that
m_{0}=n
.
Then define

\displaystyle\mathrm{maj}_{>}^{z}(T)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(r,c)\in\mu}\mathrm{sh}(r,c)\cdot|T^{z}_{>}(r,c)|,

\displaystyle\mathrm{cov}_{>}^{z}(T)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{(r,c)\in\mu}\sum_{m\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c)}\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)\Big)+\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\big(\ \ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})-\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v_{\mu}^{-1})-|T|\ \big),

where
\ell(w)
denotes the length of the permutation
w
in
S_{n}
.

2.5
Set-valued tableaux formulas for
E^{z}_{\mu}
.

The relative Macdonald polynomial
E^{z}_{\mu}=E^{z}_{\mu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)

is what is termed the “permuted basement nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomial” in
[
Al16
]

(see
[
GR21
, (3.7)]
for further references and a definition in terms of Cherednik-Dunkl operators
and the double affine Hecke algebra action on polynomials).

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
z\in S_{n}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}
. Then the relative Macdonald polynomial

E^{z}_{\mu}
is

E^{z}_{\mu}=\sum_{T}q^{\mathrm{maj}_{>}^{z}(T)}t^{\mathrm{cov}_{>}^{z}(T)}\Big(\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}\frac{1-t}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}}\Big)x^{T},

where the sum is over set-valued tableaux
T
of shape
\mu
.

Because the powers of
q
and
t
in formula in Theorem
2.1
are nonnegative integers, this formula
is well suited to the specializations
q=0
and/or
t=0
. The following Theorem provides an alternate
formula which is better for identifying the specializations of
E^{z}_{\mu}
at
q=\infty
and/or
t=\infty
(i.e.,
specializations at
q^{-1}=0
and
t^{-1}=0
).

\displaystyle\mathrm{maj}^{z}_{<}(T)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(r,c)\in\mu}\mathrm{sh}(r,c)\cdot|T^{z}_{<}(r,c)|,

\displaystyle\mathrm{cov}^{z}_{<}(T)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{(r,c)\in\mu}\sum_{m\in T^{z}_{<}(r,c)}\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)\Big)-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\big(\ \ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})-\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v_{\mu}^{-1})-|T|\ \big).

Theorem 2.2
.

Let
z\in S_{n}
and
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}
. Then the relative Macdonald polynomial

E^{z}_{\mu}
is

E^{z}_{\mu}=\sum_{T}q^{-\mathrm{maj}^{z}_{<}(T)}t^{-\mathrm{cov}^{z}_{<}(T)}\Big(\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}\frac{1-t^{-1}}{1-q^{-\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{-\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}}\Big)x^{T},

where the sum is over set-valued tableaux
T
of shape
\mu
.

The proof of Theorem
2.2
is obtained from Theorem
2.1
by multiplying
numerator and denominator of each coefficient by

\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}q^{-\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{-\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}.

The proof of Theorem
2.1
is obtained by considering the reduced word for the
n
-periodic
permutation

u_{\mu}=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}s_{u_{\mu}(r,c)-1}\cdots s_{2}s_{1}\pi\qquad\hbox{(product taken in increasing order of boxes)}

studied in
[
GR21
, Propositions 2.1 and 2.2]
(see also
[
Mac03
, (2.4.3)]
) and
using an easy bijection (see §
3.3
)

\{\hbox{alcove walks of type $\vec{u}_{\mu}$}\}\longleftrightarrow\{\hbox{set-valued tableaux of shape $\mu$}\}

This bijection gives a conversion between the alcove walks formula for
E^{z}_{\mu}

in
[
GR21
, Theorem 1.1(a)]
(see also
[
RY08
, Theorem 2.2]
for this alcove walks formula in a root system language)
and the set-valued tableaux formula in Theorem
2.1
.

Example 2.1
.

Let
n=5
and
\mu=(0,4,5,1,4)
. Then the box arrangement associated to
\mu
is as follows, with boxes placed on a labeled grid, filled with values
u_{\mu}(r,c)
, and marked in the lower corner by their cylindrical numbers
r+nc
.

(2.7)

which has size

|T|=1+1+1+2+2+1+2=10
(we shall often omit the set notation as in the right-most picture above).
The product in Theorem
2.1
corresponding to this set-valued tableau
T
is

\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}\frac{(1-t)}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\vrule\hfil\hfil\lx@intercol\\
\cline{1-4}\cr\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{4}t^{2})}&1&\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{2}t^{2})}&\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt)}\\
\hline\cr\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{5}t^{4})}&1&\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{3}t^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{3}t^{3})}&1&\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{2})}\\
\hline\cr 1\\
\cline{1-4}\cr 1&1&\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{2}t^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{2}t^{2})}&1\\
\cline{1-4}\cr\end{array}\ .

Let
z=\mathrm{id}
be the identity.

box-by-box permutation sequence of
T
with initial direction
z

(written in 1-line notation
w=(w(1)\ \cdots\ w(n))
) is

The initial and final directions of
T
are

z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}=\mathrm{id}=(12345)\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}}=z^{T}_{(3,5)}=(53142),

where
z^{T}_{(3,5)}
indicates the permutation in box
(3,5)
of
z^{T}
.
Since
v_{\mu}=(13524)
then

\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})=4+2+0+1=7\quad\text{ and }\quad\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v^{-1}_{\mu})=3,

so that

\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})-\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v^{-1}_{\mu})-|T|)=\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(7-3-10)=-3.

Then

q^{\mathrm{maj}^{z}_{>}(T)}t^{\mathrm{cov}^{z}_{>}(T)}=t^{\frac{1}{2}(7-3-10)}\cdot\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\vrule\hfil\hfil\lx@intercol\\
\cline{1-4}\cr 1&1&q^{2}t^{2}&qt\\
\hline\cr 1&1&q^{3}t^{3}&1&qt^{2}\\
\hline\cr 1\\
\cline{1-4}\cr 1&1&q^{2}t^{3}&1\\
\cline{1-4}\cr\end{array}=t^{-3}q^{9}t^{11}=q^{9}t^{8}.

3
Proof of Theorem
2.1

In this section we describe the conversion from alcove walks to set-valued tableaux.
We follow the framework of
[
GR21
, §1]
, which gives an exposition of the alcove walks formula
in the
GL_{n}
context and analyzes the favourite reduced for the
n
-periodic permutation
u_{\mu}

that is used to construct the relative Macdonald polynomial
E^{z}_{\mu}
.

3.1
Inversions and the box-greedy reduced word

An
n
-periodic permutation
is a bijection
w\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
such that

w(i+n)=w(i)+n
. Since an
n
-periodic permutation
w
is determined by the values

w(1),\ldots,w(n)
then
a permutation
w\in S_{n}
extends uniquely to an
n
-periodic permutation.
For
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
, let
s_{i}
be the transposition in
S_{n}
switching
i
and
i+1
.
Define
\pi\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
by
\pi(i)=i+1
.

An
inversion
of an
n
-periodic permutation
w
is an element of the set

\mathrm{Inv}(w)=\{(i,k)\ |\ \hbox{$i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$, $k\in\mathbb{Z}$, $i<k$ and $w(i)>w(k)$}\}.

If
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
i<j
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
then the

shift
and
height
of
(i,j+\ell n)
are defined to be

\mathrm{sh}(i,j+\ell n)=\ell\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathrm{ht}(i,j+\ell n)=j-i.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{\geq 0}

and let
u_{\mu}
be the
n
-periodic permutation defined
by

u_{\mu}(i)=v^{-1}_{\mu}(i)+n\mu_{i},\qquad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$.}

(3.1)

Let
u_{\mu}(r,c)
be as defined in (
2.2
).
Following
[
GR21
, Prop. 2.2(1)]
,
the
box-greedy reduced word for
u_{\mu}
(an abstract word in symbols
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n-1}
and
\pi)
is

u^{\square}_{\mu}=\prod_{\mathrm{boxes}\ (r,c)\ \mathrm{in}\ dg(\mu)}(s_{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\cdots s_{2}s_{1}\pi).

(3.2)

and the product is taken in increasing order of the values
r+nc
. As in
[
GR21
, (2.12)]
,
this reduced word induces a partition of the
elements of
\mathrm{Inv}(u_{\mu})
according to the boxes
(r,c)
of
\mu
, and an ordering of the
inversions in each box.
For
(r,c)\in\mu
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,u_{\mu}(r,c)\}
,

\hbox{the $i$th inversion in the box $(r,c)$ is}\qquad\beta(i,r,c)=(v_{\mu}(r),i+n\cdot(\mu_{r}-c+1)).

The inversion
\beta(i,r,c)
has shift and height

\mathrm{sh}(r,c)=\mathrm{sh}(\beta(i,r,c))=\mu_{r}-c+1\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathrm{ht}(i,r,c)=\mathrm{ht}(\beta(i,r,c))=v_{\mu}(r)-i.

(3.3)

Example 3.1
.

If
\mu=(0,4,5,1,4)
then
n=5
,
the box-greedy reduced word for
u_{\mu}
is

3.2
Alcove walks and permutation sequences

Let
z\in S_{n}
and
choose a subset
F
of the
s_{i}
factors in
u^{\square}_{\mu}=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{r}
to cross out.
The corresponding
alcove walk of type
(z,u_{\mu}^{\square})

is the sequence
p(F)=(p_{0},p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})
of
n
-periodic permutations
given by

\hbox{$p_{0}=z$}\quad\hbox{and}\quad p_{k}=\begin{cases}p_{k-1}\pi,&\hbox{if $w_{k}=\pi$},\\
p_{k-1},&\hbox{if $w_{k}\in F$ (so that the factor $w_{k}$ is crossed out)},\\
p_{k-1}w_{k},&\hbox{if $w_{k}\not\in F$ (so that the factor $w_{k}$ is not crossed out)}.\end{cases}

Following
[
GR21
, (1.14)]
,
the
permutation sequence of
p(F)
is the sequence of permutations in
S_{n}
given by

\overline{p}(F)=(\overline{p_{0}},\overline{p_{1}},\ldots,\overline{p_{r}}),\qquad\hbox{where}\quad\overline{w}(i)=w(i)\bmod n.

Note that
\overline{w_{1}w_{2}}=\overline{w_{1}}\,\overline{w_{2}}
and

\overline{\pi}=c_{n}=s_{1}\cdots s_{n-1}
and

\overline{s_{j}}=s_{j}
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.

3.3
Converting to set-valued tableaux

The bijection between alcove walks and set-valued tableaux is

\begin{matrix}\{\hbox{alcove walks of type $u^{\square}_{\mu}$}\}&\longleftrightarrow&\{\hbox{set-valued tableaux of shape $\mu$}\}\\
p(F)&\longmapsto&T\end{matrix}

where, in the set-valued tableau
T
corresponding to the alcove walk
p(F)
,
the set
T(r,c)
in box
(r,c)
specifies which factors are crossed out in that box: if a box in
T
contains
i
then
delete
s_{i}
in the corresponding word.

Example 3.2
.

In the case
\mu=(0,4,5,1,4)
, where the box-greedy reduced word for
u_{\mu}
is as given in
(
3.4
),
there are
5+2\cdot 8+3=24
factors of the form
s_{i}
in
u^{\square}_{\mu}
and
so there are a total of
2^{24}
alcove walks of type
(z,u^{\square}_{\mu})

(for any fixed permutation
z\in S_{n}
).
Each alcove walk corresponds to a choice of
s_{i}
factors in
u^{\square}_{\mu}
to cross out.

The tableau
T
in Example
2.1
corresponds to the subset

(3.6)

The
box-by-box permutation sequence

of
F
is obtained by recording the last permutation in each box, which is
z^{T}
in (
2.8
).
The elements
z^{T}_{(r,c)}
in each box of
z^{T}
are also obtained from
z=z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}
and
T
by
the formula in Section
2.3
.

We have used the blue highlighted numbers in (
3.6
)
to record the positions
i
,
i+1
when there is a repeat entry
in the sequence
\overline{p}(F)
coming from a crossed out
s_{i}
.
These record the information of
F
. This same information is translated into the underlines in
z^{T}

in (
2.8
), and the underlines exactly specify the entries of
T
.
The set-valued tableau indicating the positions of the underlined numbers in each box is the tableau in (
2.7
).

3.4
Conversion of the statistics

The proof of Theorem
2.1
is completed by matching up the statistics in Theorem
2.1

with the statistics that appear in
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]
. In Example
3.2
, we have used the
blue highlighted numbers in (
3.6
) and the underlines in (
2.8
)
to illustrate how the information of
the ‘folds’ in
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]
is equivalent to the information of the set valued tableau
T
.
In the context of
[
RY08
, (2.36)]
, the set
T(r,c)
exactly records the folds coming from the box
(r,c)
.
By (
3.3
),
the factor

\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}\frac{(1-t)}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}}

which appears in the product in Theorem
2.1
corresponds to

\hbox{the factors}\quad\left(\prod_{k\in f^{+}(p)}\frac{(1-t_{\beta^{\vee}_{k}})}{1-q^{\langle-\beta^{\vee}_{k},\rho_{c}\rangle}}\right)\left(\prod_{k\in f^{-}(p)}\frac{(1-t_{\beta^{\vee}_{k}})}{1-q^{\langle-\beta^{\vee}_{k},\rho_{c}\rangle}}\right)\quad\hbox{in \ \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{RY08}{}{}, Theorem 3.1]}.}

The sets
T^{z}_{<}
and
T^{z}_{>}
correspond to the sets
f^{+}(p)
and
f^{-}(p)
of positive and negative folds in
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]
.
The permutation
z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}}
is the permutation denoted
\varphi(p)
in
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]

(denoted
z_{r}
in
[
GR21
, (1.14)]
),
and the permutation
z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v_{\mu}^{-1}
is the permutation
m
of
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]
.
With these conversions, the last term

\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\big(\ \ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})-\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v_{\mu}^{-1})-|T|\ \big)

in the definition of
\mathrm{cov}_{>}^{z}(T)
in Section
2.5

corresponds to the combination of the
factors
t^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varphi(p)}
and
t^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\beta^{\vee}_{k}}
in
[
RY08
, Theorem 3.1]

and the factor
t^{\frac{1}{2}}_{m}
in
[
RY08
, Remark 3.2]
.

Example 3.3
.

Suppose that

n=14
,
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{14})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{14}
,
(r,c)
is a box in
\mu

and
(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})
is the box before
(r,c)
in
\mu
.
Say

u_{(r,c)}=11,\qquad T(r,c)=\{3,4,8,10\}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{8}u_{9}u_{10}u_{11}u_{12}u_{13}u_{14}),

so that
u_{i}=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}(i)
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,14\}
.
Then the box
(r,c)
of
F
contains

\displaystyle s_{11}

\displaystyle\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}s_{5}\cancel{s_{4}}\cancel{s_{3}}s_{2}s_{1}\pi=(\gamma_{3}\times\gamma_{1}\times\gamma_{4}\times\gamma_{2}\times\gamma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}\times\gamma_{1})\pi

\displaystyle=(\gamma_{3-0}\times\gamma_{4-3}\times\gamma_{8-4}\times\gamma_{10-8}\times\gamma_{12-10}\times(\gamma_{1})^{\times(14-12)})\pi

and
the entries in box
(r,c)
of the permutation sequence
\overline{p}(F)
are

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{8}u_{9}u_{10}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{8}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{5}u_{6}u_{8}u_{7}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{5}u_{8}u_{6}u_{7}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}s_{5}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}u_{4}u_{8}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}s_{5}\cancel{s_{4}}\cancel{s_{3}}s_{2}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{3}u_{2}u_{4}u_{8}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}s_{5}\cancel{s_{4}}\cancel{s_{3}}s_{2}s_{1}

\displaystyle=(u_{3}u_{1}u_{2}u_{4}u_{8}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}u_{10}u_{9}u_{12}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14})

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r,c)}

\displaystyle=(u_{1}u_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\underline{{u_{4}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\underline{{u_{8}}}}u_{5}u_{6}u_{7}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\underline{{u_{10}}}}u_{9}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\underline{{u_{12}}}}u_{11}u_{13}u_{14}{\color[rgb]{0.7,0.2,0}\bf u_{3}})

and

\displaystyle z^{T}_{(r,c)}

\displaystyle=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}s_{11}\cancel{s_{10}}s_{9}\cancel{s_{8}}s_{7}s_{6}s_{5}\cancel{s_{4}}\cancel{s_{3}}s_{2}s_{1}\gamma_{14}^{-1}

\displaystyle=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}(\gamma_{3}\times\gamma_{1}\times\gamma_{4}\times\gamma_{2}\times\gamma_{2}\times\gamma_{1}\times\gamma_{1})\gamma_{14}^{-1}

\displaystyle=z^{T}_{(r^{\prime},c^{\prime})}(\gamma_{3-0}\times\gamma_{4-3}\times\gamma_{8-4}\times\gamma_{10-8}\times\gamma_{12-10}\times(\gamma_{1})^{\times(14-12)})\gamma_{14}^{-1}

so that

z^{T}_{(r,c)}(n)=u_{3}
,

z^{T}_{(r,c)}(3)=u_{4}
,

z^{T}_{(r,c)}(4)=u_{8}
,

z^{T}_{(r,c)}(8)=u_{10}
,

z^{T}_{(r,c)}(10)=u_{12}
.
Then
T^{z}_{<}(r,c)
and
T^{z}_{>}(r,c)
are determined by

\begin{array}[]{llll}\hbox{if $u_{3}<u_{4}$\quad then}&3\in T^{z}_{<}(r,c)&\hbox{and otherwise}&3\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c);\\
\hbox{if $u_{4}<u_{8}$\quad then}&4\in T^{z}_{<}(r,c)&\hbox{and otherwise}&4\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c);\\
\hbox{if $u_{8}<u_{10}$\quad then}&8\in T^{z}_{<}(r,c)&\hbox{and otherwise}&8\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c);\\
\hbox{if $u_{10}<u_{12}$\quad then}&10\in T^{z}_{<}(r,c)&\hbox{and otherwise}&10\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c).\end{array}

4
Example: Computing
E_{(2,2,1,1,0,0)}

Here we compute
E_{(2,2,1,1,0,0)}=E^{\mathrm{id}}_{(2,2,1,1,0,0)}
using the set-valued tableaux formula
of Theorem
.
For comparison, this same Macdonald polynomial is computed in terms of nonattacking fillings
in
[

and in terms of multiline queues in
[
CMW18
, Figure 4]
.

Box by box, the corresponding factors
\frac{(1-t)}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{\mathrm{ht}(i,r,c)}}
are

\frac{(1-t)}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}}t^{\mathrm{ht}}}=\begin{array}[]{|T|T|T|T|T|}\cline{1-2}\cr\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\phantom{\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{4}t^{2})}}\end{matrix}&\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\\
\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\end{matrix}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\phantom{\frac{(1-t)}{(1-q^{4}t^{2})}}\end{matrix}&\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\\
\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\end{matrix}\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\phantom{\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt)}}\end{matrix}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{\ignorespaces$\begin{matrix}\phantom{\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt)}}\end{matrix}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}}}}}}}}}}}}}

For a set valued tableau
T
let

\mathrm{wt}(T)=q^{\mathrm{maj}_{>}^{z}(T)}t^{\mathrm{cov}_{>}^{z}(T)}\Big(\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{m\in T(r,c)}\frac{1-t}{1-q^{\mathrm{sh}(r,c)}t^{\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)}}\Big)

where

\mathrm{cov}_{>}^{z}(T)=\Big(\sum_{(r,c)\in\mu}\sum_{m\in T^{z}_{>}(r,c)}\mathrm{ht}(m,r,c)\Big)+\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\big(\ \ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{fin}})-\ell(z^{T}_{\mathrm{init}}v_{\mu}^{-1})-|T|\ \big).

For this example,
\mathrm{sh}(1,2)=\mathrm{sh}(2,2)=1
so that

\mathrm{maj}_{>}^{z}(T)=|T^{z}_{>}(r,c)|
.

E_{(2,2,1,1,0,0)}

In summary,

\displaystyle(x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4})^{-1}

\displaystyle E_{(2,2,1,1,0,0)}

\displaystyle=q^{3}

\displaystyle\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{5}x_{6}+q^{2}t\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{5}x_{6}

\displaystyle+q\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{5}x_{1}+q\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{5}x_{2}

\displaystyle+q^{2}t^{5}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{5}x_{1}+qt\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{5}x_{1}

\displaystyle+q^{2}t\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{5}x_{6}+qt\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{5}x_{2}

\displaystyle+q^{2}t\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{6}x_{5}+q^{2}t^{2}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{6}x_{5}

\displaystyle+q\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{6}x_{1}+q\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{3})}x_{6}x_{2}

\displaystyle+qt\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{1}x_{5}+qt^{2}\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{5})}x_{6}x_{5}+qt\frac{(1-t)}{(1-qt^{4})}x_{6}x_{1}+x_{1}x_{2}.
